# Forward Ledger — Open Fixes, New Features & Future Investigations

_The single forward-work tracker. **Rebuilt 2026-09-20** to forward work only (the ledger-ships pack's §1-D, landed 2026-09-22): every
live row re-verified at HEAD by the 2026-09-20 adjudication; struck rows, session notes and the
closed history moved off this file. The pre-rebuild file -- every struck row and every session
note, verbatim -- is `task-packs/FORWARD_LEDGER_PRE_REBUILD_2026-09-20.md`, a RECORD SURFACE kept
on the maintainers' `record` branch and not shipped (the 2026-08-20 rebuild's record sits beside
it). Read it for WHY a row existed; read this file for WHAT IS TRUE NOW._

**Live: 176** — 50 logic bugs · 118 hygiene · 8 operator actions.
**23** reach an adopter. Counted apart on purpose; see contract rule 5.

Every number above and in §2's population and audience tables is DERIVED from the member rows by `scripts/generate_ledger_regions.py` (a classed row inherits its class-index population and audience; a row in a class the index marks MIXED on an axis carries its own cell on that axis and repeats the class tag on the other -- §C0 is MIXED on both, and ten classes are MIXED on one as of 2026-09-20; `ledger_row.py` enforces the shape per axis) and gated by `tests/test_generate_ledger_regions.py::TestTheLiveLedgerConverges`. Derived is not verified: the tool checks that every live row carries a token it knows and counts each once; whether the token is the RIGHT one is the judgement the row's author made, contestable with `scripts/ledger_row.py repin <id> --audience ... --reason ...`. Never hand-edit a count here -- run `--write`. Before 2026-09-08 the audience figure was typed, and read 62 through 63 closures.

⚠ **This file is tracked since 2026-09-21: `git status` shows your edit and `git checkout` undoes it; the snapshots from its gitignored months are on the `record` branch.**
`scripts/record_snapshot.py` writes it to the orphan `record` branch at every handoff; restore a
lost version with `git show record:task-packs/FORWARD_LEDGER.md`. Verified 2026-09-02: the `record`
copy was byte-identical to the working file. **This corrects a warning that said "there is no git
undo", which stopped being true on 2026-08-31 when `record` landed.**
Edit by content anchor, never by replaying a line number — that rule stands on its own merit
(nine writers, shifting line numbers), not on the absent-undo claim that used to justify it.
Back it up before any structural change anyway: `record` is written at handoff, not per edit.

## The contract — what belongs in this file

- **All forward work lives here.** Do not spawn a rival tracker; update this file.
- **A row states a root cause, not a symptom.** If you are writing a row that fixes one site of a
  pattern, find its `§C` class and add it there — or open a new class. A hand-edited bandaid filed
  as its own row is how this file grew to fourteen topic buckets and 2,597 lines.
- **Stable ids are the citation handle.** `DEF-*`, `LG-*`, `INV-*`, `DEC-*`, `CLO-*` never change or
  get reused — 402 distinct (file, id) citations across `task-packs/` resolve by id. **Section numbers are not stable**;
  cite the id.
- **`§C` numbers are a fresh namespace.** The retired `§2.x`/`§3.x` numbers are mapped in Appendix A.
- **Strike a row when it lands** — the pack's own `## Landing` stanza is authoritative.
- **PRIOR TEXT is edited only to remove never-ship material** — a personal path, a
  private backup arrangement, a billing detail, a quoted person — never to change a
  claim; the verbatim original stays on the record branch, and the date and the rows go
  here. 2026-09-24 (Round 12, before the 0.8.0b1 seed): `DEF-840` and `DEF-856`.

### Carried forward verbatim from the pre-rebuild contract

_These four rules were rewritten away in the 2026-08-05 rebuild and restored after an audit found_
_the measurement missing. Rule 5 is the only thing in this file that says what its own row count_
_does and does not mean._

2. **Unlanded packs belong here.** ⚠ **This REVERSES a previous standing instruction,
   and the reversal is dated deliberately.** The operator had prohibited packs in the
   ledger; that prohibition was **withdrawn by the operator on 2026-07-30**. A
   drafted-but-unlanded pack *is* owed work, so `drafted-pack (TP-N)` and `owned: TP-N`
   rows are correct. `tests/test_forward_ledger_completeness.py::TestOrphanDetection`,
   which reds on a `Deferred/` pack with no row, is therefore enforcing the right thing.
   **Sessions carrying the old instruction in memory will otherwise strike valid rows** —
   if you are about to remove a `drafted-pack` row because "packs don't belong here",
   this line is why you should not.

3. **`LANDED` is a transitional sweep marker, never a record.** It means *"this work is
   done — remove this row on the next pass."* The permanent record lives in
   `CHANGELOG.md` and the pack's own `## Landing` stanza. `TestLandedRowLingering`
   reddening on a `LANDED` row is the sweep signal firing correctly, not a defect to
   silence.

4. **Settled decisions do not belong here.** They live in `docs/RELEASE_DECISIONS.md`,
   whose format (*Decision / Why / explicit revisit trigger*) already carries reopen
   conditions. So *"it has a reopen condition, therefore it is forward work"* does **not**
   justify a ledger row — that reasoning would re-import every decision ever made.

5. **A non-zero count is health, not backlog — but the rate below is NOT the reassurance
   it reads as.** Every landed pack emits deferrals, so draining N packs *feeds* this file
   rather than emptying it. Calibrate against the dated baseline below, not against a
   feeling about the number.

   ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-08-05 — the "~0.75 deferrals per pack" figure was a stock/flow error
   and is withdrawn.** It was computed as 41 standing rows ÷ ~55 packs. But this file holds
   only **open** rows — rule 3 removes struck ones — so rows ÷ packs measures the *residual
   after drain*, not emission. A stock divided by a flow cannot be a spawn rate.

   **Measured emission, hand-classified across all 159 packs in `Done/` (2026-08-05): 1.33
   new owed items per pack** — 77% above the withdrawn figure, and **above 1.0 in every
   era** (1.09 → 1.01 → 2.28 by chronological third). The id-counting method is confounded
   by a convention that only begins with the packs of early August 2026, so the hand-classified rubric is primary.
   Corroboration needing no classification at all: this file's own dated baseline moved
   **41 → ~137 definition sites over the 21 packs touched since 2026-07-31** — net growth
   *after* all drains.

   **What follows from a rate above 1.0:** the backlog is a divergent series and cannot be
   closed by working it. That is arithmetic, not pessimism — and it means **the count in
   this file can never be the signal that says "ready."** Two better instruments, both used
   in the 2026-08-05 assessment: (a) **adopter-facing defects fixed vs. introduced** per
   milestone — measured at roughly **6:1** over 2026-07-28 → 08-05, which is convergent;
   (b) the **blocker yield of the logic-bug population** specifically, kept separate from
   doc/registry hygiene. Reading three populations as one number is what made "we are
   nearly ready" unfalsifiable. **Both are computable now, not read from 2026-08-05:**
   `python3 scripts/ledger_trend.py` diffs this file's snapshots on the record ref
   (filed, struck, churn, net, split by audience and population, since every row
   carries both tags as of 2026-09-08), so the ratio is re-measured per window and
   the figures are deliberately NOT typed here. First run 2026-09-08: the whole
   08-31 -> 09-08 window is dominated by the 09-02 sweep that filed forty-odd
   already-owed rows in a day; read from the snapshot after it (`--from <that
   snapshot>`), replacement ran well under one row per backlog row drained.
   Re-run to re-measure. The rate itself is attacked at source by two landed packs of August 2026: one over
   pack authoring (the largest single emitter, ~33 line anchors/pack, ~1% guarded) and one
   over the codebase's hand-maintained surface.

**Baseline — measured 2026-07-31 at `4e8c809`.** An undated count becomes a false pin the
moment the file moves, so this one is dated and its method is stated: rows are counted at
their **definition site** (`- **DEF-n · …**`), not by id occurrences, because ids are
cross-referenced across sections and occurrence-counting inflates them.

⚠ **Roughly 33 of the 225 rows are already closed and still physically present** — all 21
`CLO-*` rows (§6 is a deliberate do-not-rediscover record, an *anti*-backlog), plus a handful of
`EXP-*` verdicts and discharged `LG-*`/`DEF-*` rows. **Any naive count of this file overstates
live work by ~15% before triage.** A 2026-08-05 falsification pass over 50 rows found **~12% of
checkable rows dead** (already fixed or premise falsified) and **~46% carrying at least one wrong
line number or count** — mechanisms held up far better than the numbers attached to them, which
is the same finding rule 5 records at the file level.

> **⚠ Two consequences of that last row, both learned the expensive way.** *(a)* Any
> instruction to *"remove the rows already marked struck"* has **no population** — and
> the rows that fit such a description by set-difference carry `open` / `in-progress`
> tags, i.e. **live work**. Executing it would delete live work from a file that had **no git
> undo** at the time (`task-packs/` was gitignored until 2026-09-21). *(b)* "30 live" is a **reading**, not a mechanical
> count; the tag mix above is what is actually measurable. Reclassify a row only against
> a mechanical check — the row's own claim re-run, never a re-reading of it.

**Editing rules.** This file is **tracked since 2026-09-21 (`git log -p` holds every committed state; the `record` branch holds the snapshots from before)** (see the
header — the "no git undo" this line used to assert has been false since 2026-08-31). Apply every
edit by **content anchor against the live file**, never by replaying a cited line number. Do not
run a parallel session that edits it. Nine packs currently write to it, so ordering
matters: this contract lands first, everyone else appends and strikes per it, and any
structural consolidation lands last — after every append — or it restructures a file that
is then appended to again.


- **Governing frame:** Espalier is a *workshop toolbelt, not a security boundary* — the threat model
  is operator/AI *mistakes*, and false-positives / workflow friction outrank closing bypass classes.
  And *direction ≠ magnitude*: a sound mechanism is not a measured effect until it is measured.

### How this file defends itself

_Three mechanisms, added by the 2026-08-20 rebuild. Each exists because the
corresponding failure was **measured** on this file, not anticipated._

**1. Derived regions — counts are generated, never typed.** The §2 headline (all three of its numbers), the class
index, the per-class member counts, Appendix B, the top-of-file split and adopter figure and the §2 population and audience tables are all computed from the member rows
at generation time. Strike, file and re-pin rows with `scripts/ledger_row.py strike|file|repin`, never by hand: every verb drives the row's probe first, writes both files atomically or neither, and converges these regions. Three sections had been mis-stating their own member count
simultaneously (§C1 said "20 open, 9 closed" over 30 rows; §C2 "Members (19)" over 20;
§C18 "Members (4)" over 6). A hand-typed count in this file is a §C11 defect at birth.
**Never hand-edit a count here — regenerate:**

```bash
python3 scripts/generate_ledger_regions.py --check   # what disagrees with the member rows
python3 scripts/generate_ledger_regions.py --write   # repair the literal substitutions
```


**2. Probes — every row can re-derive its own claim.** `task-packs/LEDGER_PROBES.json`
holds a read-only one-liner per row plus the value it prints *while the defect is open*.
Run them:

```bash
python3 scripts/check_ledger_probes.py
```

A row whose probe stops printing its `open_value` is a **STRIKE CANDIDATE** — re-verify by
hand, then strike. This replaces the nine-agent census that found 41 dead rows; the same
answer is now one command. ⚠ **The verdict is three-way on purpose.** A probe whose
`subject` file is missing reports UNRESOLVED, never STRIKE: an early cut of the runner read
"the probe stopped saying True" as "the defect is fixed" when the truth was "the probe lost
its subject". A wrong strike is expensive rather than fatal — the file was gitignored until 2026-09-21, but
`record` carries it (see the header) — and the three-way verdict stands regardless: re-discovering
a strike you cannot see is work, and UNRESOLVED is the honest answer when the probe lost its
subject. ⚠ **A struck row must also LOSE its probe.** The gate only required every live row to
HAVE one, so retired probes accumulated: on 2026-09-02, 14 of 198 pointed at struck rows and 10 of
11 "strike candidates" were that noise. `scripts/generate_ledger_regions.py` now reports the other
direction too.

**3. Symbol anchors over line numbers.** ~70% of the pre-rebuild `file:line` anchors no
longer landed on their mechanism (the 2026-08-05 pass measured 46% — the rot accelerates).
Anchors are now `file::symbol` or `file#heading` wherever one exists: forms this repo can
already validate, since `tests/test_doc_source_citations.py` AST-resolves the first and
`tests/test_md_heading_anchors.py` the second. **A bare line number is a last resort, not
the convention** — and it is the one anchor form nothing here can check.


## How to read a status tag

| Tag | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `owed` | Must happen before a confident OSS launch. |
| `drafted-pack` | A `TP-*` pack is written and execution-ready but has not run. |
| `idea-only` | Named and reasoned-about; no pack drafted yet. |
| `in-progress` | Partially built — one wave landed, the rest deferred. |
| `parked` | Deliberately held behind a named revisit trigger (stated in the row). |
| `open` | An identified defect with no pack yet (the default state of a §C member). |

## §1 — Launch gates (1 day-one defect + 6 operator actions)

**The public cut is RED until both blocks below are done.** They are different work: §1A is code
and docs a stranger trips over on day one; §1B is the operator ritual that has to happen in order.

### §1A — a stranger hits these on day one

Each was verified to break someone who clones, `pip install`s, runs `fuse`, or follows the README.
`○` marks a row no local check can close.

| # | id | issue | site | root cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 ○ | `LG-14` `LG-3` | GitHub's private-vulnerability-reporting setting is an owed pre-flip operator action; until it is on, SECURITY.md's preferred `Report a vulnerability` link and config.yml's contact_link both 404. | `SECURITY.md:15` | — |

### §1B — the operator launch sequence (do in this order)

No code fix exists for any of these. **Order matters:** branch protection must follow the
check-run rehearsal (§C8) or every PR wedges on a status check that never reports; and the flip
must precede the PyPI publish, because `espalier init` prints a `blob/main` CHEAT-SHEET URL that
404s for every adopter while the repo is private.

⚠ **The stated order above is wrong until step 0 clears, and that is why this note lives here rather than
only in the row.** Measured 2026-08-15 (`DEF-582`/`LG-18`): every job of all five push-triggered workflows
at `42c8d83` failed in 2-4s with `steps=0` while Actions was unavailable, so CI is a no-op. That removes the
precondition of step 5 (§C8's rehearsal cannot establish a check-run name when no job starts), of step 4
(`refresh-externals` cannot run at all), and of the publish leg that steps 1 and 3 exist to reach
(`publish.yml`'s tag-push trigger fires into nothing). **Steps 2 and 6 — flip the repo public, record the
demo GIF — are the only two that do not depend on it,** together with the local half of step 1 (version
bump plus CHANGELOG fold). Do not read "4 of 6" as "mostly fine": step 5 done in this state wedges every
PR on main with certainty rather than as a `DEF-455` hazard.



⚠ **`DEC-31`, 2026-09-07 — the seed is unchanged; what happens after it is not: the public repo
becomes the development tree, and the forward work ships in it.** Decided after measuring the
alternative DEC-25 left open, a private layer for the untracked work: the record branch already
exists and the handoff already writes it, yet 31 of its 49 snapshots were unpushed on 2026-09-07
because the push is a separate step, so a second system had already failed silently. Under
DEC-31: (1) `scripts/build_release_archive.py` still emits the seed, and the fresh-repo reasons
in `memory/publish-from-a-generated-public-repo.md` stand unchanged, since they are about
history; (2) after the seed, development moves into the public repo, and this tree, renamed,
becomes a read-only private archive and the host of the record branch through a second remote;
(3) the forward ledger, its probes file and the active top-level packs are tracked and public,
rebuilt first to forward work only (`DEF-705`), while Done, Deferred, Merged, Scrapped, the
archive files, blueprints and reports stay untracked as regenerable history; (4)
`docs/known-findings.md` folds into the ledger (`DEF-706`); (5) the shipping boundary and the
codename gate move with it (`DEF-707`, `DEF-708`) and the DEC-25 texts are rewritten
(`DEF-709`). What must never ship is a short list, mostly mechanical: the dropped-disclosure
name (under Done only), the CI-outage story (two shipping docs carry it today), personal
paths, and any row written as a war story instead of a claim. The ongoing discipline is one the
memory directory and the failure-mode catalog already ship under: every row is written for a
stranger. Order: the adopter-facing lanes not gated on the Windows walk, the walk re-anchor
(`DEF-711`) and the walk, the §C5 trio, then §C51 and `DEF-709` at the start of the
release-repo phase, then the runbook.

## §2 — Open fixes, by unit of work (176 LIVE issues in 29 classes + 81 standalone)

_Rebuilt 2026-08-20 from a **full census**: every live row re-verified at HEAD by nine
independent agents, then re-classified twice independently (98.5% pairwise agreement).
**Classes are units of WORK, not diagnoses** — members share one provable acceptance rule (one oracle, one method), not always one edit (several classes' own "Why one class" paragraphs say the repairs differ per member), so a pack scoped to a
class can honestly report it closed. The previous structure grouped by symptom; eight of its
nine stated "one fix"es were falsified when driven._


**The three populations are counted apart — never as one number.** Merging them is what
made "we are nearly ready" unfalsifiable (contract rule 5).

| `DEF-675` | scripts/check_handoff_landing.py::SELECTION (:50); missing member tests/test_contracts.py::TestMemoryMdLineLimit | The post-handoff gate omits the one contract that governs the file every handoff writes: `scripts/check_handoff_landing.py::SELECTION` names thirteen modules and `CONDITIONAL` two more, and `tests/test_contracts.py` -- the sole home of the 120-line cap on `ESPALIER_MEMORY.md`, at `tests/test_contracts.py::TestMemoryMdLineLimit::test_memory_md_within_cap` -- is in neither list. The gate exists because every handoff step writes after the session's last full verification, so the only oracle for the one failure mode definitionally about that file's size is the full suite. Who: the maintainer finishing a handoff, who appends session-log rows, sees the gate pass in seconds, and learns the cap broke from a full suite run minutes later. Fix: add `tests/test_contracts.py::TestMemoryMdLineLimit` -- the class, not the module, which is large -- to `SELECTION`. | minor | HYGIENE | MAINTAINER |
| `DEF-674` | tools/cc/hooks/session_start.py::_build_context (`_MAX_CONTEXT_BYTES` = 16000, :60); gate tests/test_session_banner.py::TestBannerContracts::test_banner_stays_lean | The SessionStart banner's bounded tenants can together exceed the lean gate that guards them: their caps alone (goal 4500 + memory 1200 + standing 1000 + blueprint 6000 = 12700 bytes) plus the unbounded header and footgun pointer (2575 bytes at HEAD) sum to 15275, over both the composed ceiling `test_banner_stays_lean` builds (15200) and its second assertion at 0.9 x `_MAX_CONTEXT_BYTES` (14400). The tenants that grow are the ones that should grow -- one line per ratified standing principle, one per session in the memory digest, and the blueprint accumulating toward its 6KB cap by design -- so the squeeze is structural and recurs, while the cheapest response, trimming whichever tenant is nearest, evicts context the next session opens on. The banner-health advisory reports a total byte count only, naming neither the tenant that is over its own budget nor the headroom left. Who: a maintainer whose correct and desirable edit to a banner-fed doc reds an unrelated gate at the end of a full suite run. Fix: have the advisory report per-tenant budget against actual plus remaining headroom, and render the standing-principles index as a summary line rather than one line per principle. | minor | HYGIENE | MAINTAINER |
| `DEF-671` | scripts/check_ledger_probes.py::run_probe (the contamination gate) + ::_contaminating_trees (`_DUPE_SCAN_LIMIT`, :116) + ::_ignored_root_dirs | Three limits in the probe contamination gate discard a live probe's verdict over a duplicate that probe could not have counted. `run_probe` intersects the duplicate's suffixes with the suffixes a probe names but never reads the root the probe walks, so a probe rooted at `tests/` is gated by a duplicate in a root-level staging directory it cannot reach; `_contaminating_trees` breaks its per-directory scan at `_DUPE_SCAN_LIMIT` (4000, `scripts/check_ledger_probes.py::_DUPE_SCAN_LIMIT`) with no signal to the caller, so a clean tree and an abandoned scan report alike; and `_ignored_root_dirs` inspects only root-level directories git ignores, so a duplicate under a nested ignored path, or in an untracked-but-not-ignored directory, is invisible while its docstring calls root level the only place a duplicate can hide. All three fail closed, costing a verdict rather than producing a wrong strike. Who: the maintainer re-deriving the ledger after a build has run, who gets UNRESOLVED on most tree-walking rows and re-verifies each by hand. Fix: carry each probe's walk root into the gate and intersect on root as well as suffix, report a scan-limit break as an explicit unknown, widen the ignored-directory census past root level, and correct the docstring. | minor | LOGIC_BUG | MAINTAINER |
| ~~`DEF-672`~~ | docs/RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md#First publish (one-time) — seeds a NEW public repo; memory/publish-from-a-generated-public-repo.md | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-09-23 — closed 2026-09-23 by the M4 pack *the console, then the cut* (TP-456 4-C-4), each of the four pieces re-derived against `docs/RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md` §First publish as it reads today rather than as this row remembered it. (1) The freshness re-pin deadline is stated twice: step 1 ("re-pin before seeding if this returns anything") and the "Re-pin the cohort at the cut" section ("before tagging") -- present, no edit. (2) The provenance note keeping the vulnerability-reporting runbook out of a shipping document: `espalier.surface_contract.classify_release_path('docs/RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md')` returns `internal`, so the document does not ship and the note has nothing to keep out -- moot by classification, no edit. (3) The authorship note was genuinely absent: step 5 committed with whatever identity the shell's git config held, on a decision whose subject is identity disclosure. Added: two `git config --local user.name` / `user.email` lines and a read-back, in the seed block before `git branch -M main`. (4) The stray-lightweight-tag detector: **refuted, not satisfied.** `DEC-31` (2026-09-07) retired the sweep -- what leaves this tree is the record branch, never `main` and never a tag -- and step 7 now says so in as many words, naming `pre-pack-TP-104` as a KEEP. This row's probe keyed on a `for-each-ref ... refs/tags` command being present, so a green would have required adding a command the checklist tells the reader not to run; the probe is retired with the row rather than satisfied. The cross-reference at the top of the section ("the evidence behind every warning below") now points at a memory doc that carries an "Evidence behind the runbook's warnings (driven 2026-09-02)" section, so the narrowing the row asked for has happened in the target rather than in the pointer.** PRIOR TEXT: Four pieces of one-way-door procedure are absent from both the first-publish runbook and the memory doc that carries its reasoning: the freshness re-pin deadline (neither file states one), the provenance note keeping the private-vulnerability-reporting runbook out of a document that ships to adopters, an authorship note on the seeded initial commit (the step commits with whatever identity the operator's git config holds, on a decision whose whole subject is identity disclosure), and the stray-lightweight-tag detector command the surviving text still implies a sweep for. The checklist also tells the reader at `docs/RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md:457` that the memory doc holds 'the evidence behind every warning below' while that file carries nothing for several of them. Who: the operator running the first publish once, where each loss is paid at the moment it cannot be undone. Fix: re-derive each of the four and place it in whichever document owns it, then narrow the cross-reference to the warnings the memory doc actually evidences. | minor | HYGIENE | OPERATOR |
| `DEF-721` | pyproject.toml::[build-system] + ::[tool.setuptools.packages.find]; MANIFEST.in; witness tests/test_wheel_payload.py::test_sdist_python_members_are_all_tracked | The wheel and the sdist -- the two artifacts that reach PyPI -- are enumerated from the filesystem rather than the git index, so an untracked `.py` lying under a swept package directory at build time ships. `pyproject.toml::[build-system]` requires only `setuptools>=77.0` with no `setuptools_scm`, `::[tool.setuptools.packages.find]` includes `espalier*`, and `MANIFEST.in` sweeps `espalier`, `tools`, `docs`, `examples`, `memory` and `bench` by recursive pattern. `tests/test_wheel_payload.py::test_sdist_python_members_are_all_tracked` requires every Python member of a built artifact to be tracked, which is an artifact-level witness rather than a fix. Who: the maintainer cutting a release from a working tree that holds a scratch module, who publishes it. Fix: enumerate the index at build time -- `setuptools_scm`'s file finder, or a manifest derived from the git index -- and keep the witness as the second check. | minor | LOGIC_BUG | MAINTAINER |
| `DEF-722` | tools/cc/hooks/post_write_check.py::main (checks are ::check_json, ::check_placeholders, ::check_python_syntax); witness docs/SHARP_EDGES.md#A missing `write_guard.py` wedges the whole session, and the fix is not reachable from inside it | A hook edit that leaves a sibling module's attribute dangling -- renaming a helper in one write and adding the compatibility wrapper in the next -- makes the live `write_guard` raise on every tool call between the two writes, and `main`'s crash guard turns that into a fail-closed deny on every mutation tool, including the Bash call that would run mypy, so the session cannot repair itself. Nothing fires at the moment of the edit: `post_write_check.py` runs `check_json`, `check_placeholders` and `check_python_syntax`, none of which re-resolves a cross-module attribute; `mypy tools/cc/hooks/` catches the dangling name only at proof time; and the sharp-edges entry is a convention, not a mechanism. Who: a maintainer, or an adopter in maintenance mode, editing a hook helper mid-session. Fix: a non-blocking PostToolUse advisory beside `check_python_syntax` that, when the written path is under `tools/cc/hooks/`, AST-collects every `<module>.<attr>` reference to the edited module from its sibling hooks and prints the attributes that no longer resolve; it fires before the next tool call is denied. The new detector self-collides with the deny-token scanners, so it needs its own lane. | minor | LOGIC_BUG | MAINTAINER |
| `DEF-755` | tools/cc/hooks/_hook_utils.py::_msys_drive_to_windows (three call sites, no census over the normalisers that must call it); out-of-scan site tools/cc/_paths.py::_repo_root | The rule that every hook path normaliser translates the Git Bash drive prefix on both sides of a compare has no mechanical gate. Three call sites exist at HEAD -- `tools/cc/hooks/_hook_utils.py::_project_root_spelling`, `::_clean_path_prefixes` and `tools/cc/hooks/_bash_patterns.py::_posix` -- the rule is written into `docs/SHARP_EDGES.md#Path Normalization on Windows`, and nothing fires when a fourth site appears without the call: `tools/cc/sister_site_probe.py` is name-keyed, so a normaliser that merely omits the call duplicates nothing; no census enumerates these functions; and no `# msys-drive: ok` annotation exists anywhere in the tree. One site already sits outside the scan the rule's review proposed -- `tools/cc/_paths.py::_repo_root` resolves `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` raw, one directory above `tools/cc/hooks/` -- and the divergence between the two root resolvers is owned by `DEF-664`; this row is the gate. Who: a Windows adopter in Git Bash on the day a new normaliser lands without the call, with no test to say so. Fix: an AST scan over `tools/cc/**/*.py` -- not `tools/cc/hooks/*.py` -- for a function that calls `os.path.realpath`, `.resolve()` or `.relative_to()` on a parameter, requiring either a `_msys_drive_to_windows` call in the same function or a `# msys-drive: ok <reason>` annotation, seeded with the three sites plus `tools/cc/hooks/config_guard.py::_scan_payload`, which fails safe on the spelling and says so inline. Most candidate sites are a hook resolving its own files rather than an agent's path, so the gate needs a design pass to name the mutation precisely, and it should extend a check that already runs rather than add one that must be invoked. | minor | HYGIENE | MAINTAINER |

Who: the next maintainer who adds a read-back, a stat or a prune after a normaliser and ships a hook that acts on the root's copy of a worktree file. Fix: a contract test over `tools/cc/hooks/*.py` that finds every `<dir> / <rel>` join where `<rel>` was bound from a `normalize_*` or `resolve_*` call and requires `<dir>` to be the base that call returned, waiving the by-construction root-keyed sites with the existing `# sister-site: ok purpose-scoped: <reason>` marker shape (`_protected_zones.py` prefix rels, `_integrity.py` manifest rels). | minor | HYGIENE | MAINTAINER |
| `DEF-736` | espalier/cli.py::_deploy_managed_py (the `skipped_user_file` branch) + ::deploy_harness (the hook-drift warning, :2587) | On the harness's own tree every deployed script takes the `skipped_user_file` branch and earns a warning that asserts a difference the bytes refute. `_deploy_managed_py` compares the deployed file against source-plus-injected-marker and, finding a mismatch with no managed marker present, returns `skipped_user_file`; `deploy_harness` then prints that the file 'differs from packaged version; preserving user edit' and advises adding a marker. Driven at HEAD on a clean clone: all 38 deployed scripts classify as user files, while `tools/cc/hooks/write_guard.py` hashes byte-identical to its deploy source -- the real condition is the absent marker, not a difference. Who: a maintainer or contributor running `init` on a fresh clone, reading 38 warnings whose stated cause the hashes contradict, which is the noise that teaches an operator to skip warnings. | `DEF-665` | pyproject.toml::[tool.pytest.ini_options] (:103-104); 43 call sites under tests/, e.g. tests/test_doctor.py, tests/test_marker_parity.py, tests/test_merge_settings.py | **Forty-three subprocess call sites under `tests/` pass a `timeout` larger than the 60-second pytest-timeout ceiling that fires first, and the ceiling's `thread` method turns one slow test into a dead session -- which it did, three times, the first day CI ran again.** Each site states a budget that cannot be reached and the `TimeoutExpired` branch written beside it is unreachable. `pyproject.toml::[tool.pytest.ini_options]` sets `timeout = 60` with `timeout_method = "thread"`, so the ceiling kills the whole pytest process with a stack dump instead of failing the one call, and a single hung -- or merely slow -- test presents as a dead suite with no summary line. First live bite, 2026-09-23, the first runs after the 2026-09-10 switch-off: `tests/test_declared_limits_contract.py::test_every_cited_pin_runs_and_passes_on_this_host` (37 s on the self-host box, one pytest child over the cited population) crossed 60 s on the 2-core runners and ended the session in the push CI's `test (3.10)` and `clean-checkout (3.12)` cells, and in the Portability macOS and Windows cells, at about two percent of the suite -- so the Windows real-host rows never ran and a third red (`tests/test_host_check.py`, a bare remote whose default branch the runner names differently) was seen only on the one cell that survived. Who: a maintainer debugging a stalled run, who reads a per-call budget the runner will never honour and gets a process-wide dump rather than one test's timeout; and, measured now, the operator reading a release tree's CI as red with no test named. Fix shape: `timeout_method = "signal"` where the platform has `SIGALRM`, so the ceiling fails the one test and the run reports the rest (keep `thread` only where `signal` is unavailable); `--durations=25` on the CI pytest lines (`test.yml`, `portability.yml`) as the cheap precursor -- the per-host distribution the local tier structurally cannot see; lower each over-ceiling site below the ceiling, or mark the tests that genuinely need longer (the per-test `@pytest.mark.timeout(N)` the 2026-09-23 fix used) and derive the population so a new site above the ceiling reds. The probe counts the over-ceiling call sites and closes when none remain. | major | HYGIENE | MAINTAINER |

Two deviations from the fix shape, stated: the contract lives beside the existing wall-clock detector in `tests/test_proof_tier.py` rather than in `tests/test_test_suite_contract.py` (the loader and the file list were already there), and the chain rows share one tier keyed on their floor band rather than one constant each. The matrix re-read goes on the release commit, once.** PRIOR TEXT: **Every wall-clock ceiling in the three serial timing files is sized by hand against a floor measured on the self-host box, and nothing derives the margin -- so each one is found, one shared-runner read at a time.** The first day CI ran again (2026-09-23) read four of them wrong on the release tree, none a regression: `tests/test_speedbump_irreversible.py`'s 32 KB flood rows at a 50 ms design budget (30.7 ms here, 50.6 on the runner); `tests/test_redos.py::test_the_delete_walk_stays_linear_through_the_chain` and the two var-expansion pre-pass rows at the regex rows' 1 s budget over walks whose floor is hundreds of milliseconds; the walker scaling row's flat arm, whose additive 1 ms noise model met a per-call cache rebuild of over a hundred on a slow host; and `_WALKER_CEILING_MS` itself at twice its floor, which a runner exceeded by more than three times the box. Each was fixed at its site by the same rule the file already states for its regex rows (`tests/test_redos.py::_CI_SAFE_BUDGET_MS`, whose own comment records the rule and its date): the design budget stays documented, the asserted ceiling is ten times the recorded floor, the alarm is the runaway detector and the scaling arm the shape detector. The rule is applied at five sites by hand and pinned at none: the next timing row written against this box's floor ships with twice-the-floor again and is found the same way. A fifth site the next day (2026-09-24), on the closing witness of the last pre-cut unit: the matrix dispatch's `clean-checkout (3.14)` cell (run `35954967897`) read the `quoted_literal_flood` row of `tests/test_redos.py::test_ps_program_body_linear_on_unclosed_runs` red at the shared `_CI_SAFE_BUDGET_MS` line -- a row that drives the whole PowerShell extractor, not one regex, with a floor of 335 ms on this box (linear: 78, 163, 336 ms at 7.5, 15, 30 KB), so the shared 1 s line is three times its floor, not ten; the alarm fired inside `_ps_dotnet_paths`. The same commit's push set, its five `test` cells and its 3.11 clean-checkout cell passed, and fail-fast cancelled the 3.10, 3.12 and 3.13 clean-checkout cells, so the matrix read three of its five clean-checkout cells not at all. The site fix is the rule applied a sixth time -- its own named ceiling at ten times a dated floor, with a scaling arm as the shape detector; the fix shape below stands. Who: the maintainer reading a release tree's CI as red on a row that measures the runner, not the tree, four times in one day. Fix shape: a contract in `tests/test_test_suite_contract.py` (or beside the two ceiling constants) that enumerates every `elapsed_ms <`/`setitimer` bound in the three `--ignore`d serial files and requires each to be a named constant no less than ten times a named, dated floor recorded beside it -- derived from the files, never a roster; and `docs/FAILURE_MODES.md`'s wall-clock entry gains the runner multiplier measured today (about 1.65x this box on ordinary rows, over 3x on one). No local oracle for the rule until that contract exists; the probe is the contract's own name once it does. | major | HYGIENE | MAINTAINER |

Driven on CPython 3.10 with tomli 1.0.0: `load()` is typed `(fp: TextIO, *, parse_float)` and a binary handle raises `TypeError: a bytes-like object is required, not str`, which the site's `except (OSError, _tomllib.TOMLDecodeError)` does not catch. The loader opens and parses BEFORE reading any key, so any `espalier.toml` trips it, not only one declaring `plan_exempt_prefixes`. `_check_path`/`_check_rel` call `_is_exempt`, which reaches the loader on every path the harness-exempt prefixes do not claim, so the `TypeError` rides up to `plan_guard.main`'s fail-closed `except BaseException` and becomes `PLAN_GUARD_INTERNAL_ERROR`: a deny of every Write/Edit/NotebookEdit on adopter source. Who is hurt: that adopter, editing their own source, meeting `plan_guard internal error; failing closed` and an audit record naming only the exception class. Fix shape: raise the floor to `tomli>=2.0`, whose `load()` takes a binary handle, or widen the site's except to catch `TypeError` and fall back to strict mode per the function's own "Never raises" contract; `espalier/_vendor/cc/hooks/plan_guard.py` is byte-identical at HEAD and takes the same edit. The probe keys on both remedies at once: True while the parsed `project.dependencies` floor is under 2.0 AND no handler in that function names `TypeError`, so either honest fix flips it; a fix keeping both values re-pins the row. | `DEF-895` | espalier/red_team_guard.py::_is_specific_match (the empty-string net `return compiled.search("") is None`); espalier/red_team_guard.py::_CATCH_ALL_MATCHES; espalier/red_team_guard.py::verify_repro (the `re.search(match_field, output)` test) and ::guard_findings (the per-blocker gate); consumer scripts/run_pack_chain.sh (the `guard_findings` receipt); enforcer tests/test_red_team_guard.py::TestVerifyRepro | **A one-character catch-all signature clears the red-team guard's specificity check, so a repro that failed for an unrelated reason is recorded as a verified blocker.** `espalier/red_team_guard.py::_is_specific_match` rejects a signature only when it is non-string or blank, is one of the six literals in `::_CATCH_ALL_MATCHES`, does not compile, or re.search-matches the empty string (`compiled.search("") is None`) -- so a single non-empty character class (a bare dot, `[^q]`, a word class) passes all four: no literal entry, no match on the empty string, yet firing on the first character of any output. **Unit of work.** Sit at the GitHub/PyPI consoles and do them in this order: claim the espalier-harness PyPI slug and add environment protection rules to the pypi environment; enable 'Actions may create and approve pull requests' so the weekly externals-drift job can open its PR (three months of unreviewed hook-protocol drift is sitting on branches nobody sees); fold the [Unreleased] CHANGELOG section (thousands of lines -- derive the count, it drifts) and bump pyproject to 0.8.0b1; seed the NEW public repository from the generated tree (DEC-25/DEC-31 -- `docs/RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md` §First publish; this tree is never flipped public: its releases carried private working material, since removed, `DEF-840`); record the demo GIF. (Rewritten 2026-09-20: until then this sentence still read "flip repo visibility to public", the event `DEC-25`/`DEC-31` foreclose -- the sibling site `memory/a-retraction-does-not-propagate-itself.md` did not list.)


**What this does NOT reach.** It does not make the release CORRECT - the documented tag-push sequence is still wrong (see drive-the-release-and-contribution-procedures-literally, which must land first or the tag push silently publishes nothing).


**Why one class.** No code change exists for any member; the census population is OPERATOR_ACTION for all five. They are one class because they are one sitting at two web consoles plus one screen recording, and because every other class in this ledger is unshippable until they are done.

<a id="c2"></a>

### §C2 — Walk the documented release and contribution procedures step by step against the live workflow and correct every step that fails

**Unit of work.** Open docs/RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md, CONTRIBUTING.md and .github/workflows/publish.yml side by side and execute the sequence on paper: replace the three bulk-tag-push sites with a single-tag push (and move tests/test_release_checklist_contract.py:56, which currently REQUIRES the defective command); move the namespace/environment step above the tag push and fix the attribution sentence naming a trigger the workflow does not listen for; add a Recovery subsection (or a workflow_dispatch with a tag input) so a swallowed release has a route back; re-base the dry-run rehearsal on the current pyproject version so it can reach the handshake it exists to exercise; correct the digest recipe to resolve the COMMIT sha rather than the annotated tag object's, and re-pin the four v1.14.0 prose sites to the live v1.14.2; name pre-pack-TP-104 as a keep in the delete-every-lightweight-tag step (that tag is what keeps a security test from silently skipping); and in CONTRIBUTING add the approval marker to the Hook Rules section and the PR template, add the missing tag-push step with a cross-reference to the checklist, and change both plain-tag sites to annotated tags.


**What this does NOT reach.** It does not publish anything (ship-blockers-only-the-operator-can-clear) and it does not touch publish.yml's own version-guard logic beyond adding a recovery entry point.


**Why one class.** Every member is found and closed by the SAME act: reading one documented instruction and asking whether following it literally produces the stated result. Three members (DEF-448, DEF-452, DEF-460) are a single causal chain - the bulk tag push swallows the release, the namespace does not exist yet, and there is no way back - so a pack that fixes one and not the others leaves the trap armed. The two CONTRIBUTING release steps are the same sequence written in a second file.

### §C13 — Build the release archive from the git index and give the transient predicate one owner

**Unit of work.** In espalier/release_pack.create_release_zip enumerate the git index instead of safe_rglob, so an untracked-but-not-gitignored file that classifies public cannot ship; add a bare .git FILE entry to release_noise.TRANSIENT_FILES - in a worktree or submodule checkout .git is a text file holding a local absolute gitdir path and the contract currently classifies it as shippable; and make pre_release._find_transient_noise call surface_contract.is_transient as its sibling already does, so the two halves of one gate stop disagreeing.


**What this does NOT reach.** It does not change the release REPORT's accounting (count-what-the-release-walk-pruned) and it does not validate the built artifact (make-each-release-gate-fail-closed-on-what-it-validates).


**Why one class.** All three are the same walk-and-classify path disagreeing with itself: two enumerators, two transient predicates. One oracle closes all three - build the zip from a git worktree checkout that contains an untracked public file, and diff the archive members against the git index.


_Both independent reclassification passes grouped these members._


**Members (1)** — derived, never typed


| id | site | what | sev |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DEF-634` | `.gitattributes` (no `export-ignore` row for `memory/CONVERGENCE_LEDGER.md`); oracle `espalier/surface_contract.py::classify_release_path` | `memory/CONVERGENCE_LEDGER.md` is an internal convergence record that reaches the generated public tree: it is tracked, `classify_release_path` returns `public` for it, it is absent from `_LOCAL_ONLY_PATHS`, and `git check-attr export-ignore` reports `unspecified`, so neither the classifier nor the archive attribute holds it back — the defect is that a record is in the public export set at all, not that a pointer inside it has aged. Who: the maintainer emitting the generated public repo, where DEC-25's one-way door makes a shipped internal record unrecoverable once pushed, and the stranger who reads 435 lines of internal convergence verdicts on launch day. Fix shape: give the file the `_LOCAL_ONLY_PATHS` entry and the `export-ignore` row its sibling records carry, derived from the declared record-surface set rather than typed one path at a time. **Decided 2026-09-21 in the shipping-boundary lane: internal, out of the public tree.** The footprint is the standard one for a tracked internal doc (`_INTERNAL_FILENAME_PATTERNS`, `MANIFEST.in`, `.gitattributes`, `EXPORT_IGNORED_INTERNAL_DOCS`) plus six test modules that read the file by name (`test_documented_claims`, `test_maintenance_mode`, `test_workflow_schedule_claims`, `test_protected_path_contract_parity`, `test_recall`, `test_doc_source_citations`) and the pull-recall corpus, which indexes it as a record: the public checkout's corpus would differ from this tree's, so the pasted recall-evaluation tables need re-measuring on both sides. Execution is owed to its own lane with those tables as the oracle, after `DEF-868`'s re-measurement; nothing in this lane moved the file. | minor | HYGIENE | MAINTAINER |

### §C18 — Resolve every reference in shipped text against the deploy inventory, then require or delete each target

**Unit of work.** Build one resolver that takes a reference out of shipped text - a path, a section, a test node-id, a symbol - and answers whether the adopter's tree receives it; run it over the deployed docs, the CLI help/report strings, .claude/commands/, tools/cc/session_summary.py and _reinject.py, and for each of the 65 dead test-path cites and 6 live help/report cites either ship the target or delete the cite; wire the same resolver into scripts/sync_asset_docs.py so no wikilink and no reference to an unshipped doc can re-enter; and fix the one shipped module docstring that names a class that does not exist (the nearest real symbol is ReflectFinding).


**What this does NOT reach.** It does not fix a shipped SENTENCE that misdescribes behaviour (drive-each-verb-and-rewrite-the-sentence-that-describes-it) - only references to targets the adopter's tree never receives.


**Why one class.** One resolver, three consumers. DEF-553 is 86 sister sites of exactly the thing DEF-412i's gate would have stopped, and DEF-392a is the same population at symbol granularity. The census records that DEF-553's count GREW rather than shrank, which is the evidence that per-site fixing does not close it.

### §C25 — Bring the workflow asset install-ci copies into every adopter repo under the same governance as the source workflow

### §C26 — Make the three release gates fail closed: right artifact, all stages, content floor

**Unit of work.** In scripts/wheel_smoke.py select the wheel by PEP-440 version rather than lexicographically - a 0.8.0a13 build silently loses to a leftover 0.8.0a9 and the test validates the stale artifact; in scripts/final_release_matrix.py record skipped stages and require all six ran before reporting 'ready to tag'; in scripts/release_check.py hoist the required-content floor and apply it at both call sites, so archive validation stops blessing a near-empty release ZIP because it only ever looks for bad files.


**What this does NOT reach.** It does not change what any gate checks FOR - only that it checked, and checked the right artifact.


**Why one class.** All three return PASS without having examined the thing they exist to examine. One oracle for the class: run each gate against a deliberately-bad artifact - a stale wheel, a run with stages skipped, an empty zip - and require red. Three scripts, one sitting, one acceptance criterion.

Who: the maintainer running the wheel smoke against a `dist/` that was not emptied, who is told the current build passed when a stale artifact was the one installed and exercised. | `DEF-652` | bench/run_benchmark.py::check_pass_conditions | The release-gating benchmark's pass conditions refuse an empty in-scope corpus but raise no objection to an empty out-of-scope corpus, so half the contract can go unevaluated and still report a clean pass. Driven at HEAD: the call with `oos_total=0` returns an empty objection list, while the same call with `in_scope_total=0` returns "refusing to pass a gate with nothing to verify". Who: the maintainer running the benchmark after a corpus edit or a change to the in-scope/out-of-scope filter, who reads a clean table and exit 0 while the false-positive half was never measured. Fix shape: apply the same zero-population refusal to the out-of-scope total that the function already applies to the in-scope total. | minor |

### §C27 — Make every sync command leave the tree converged and warn on both sides of a mirror

**Unit of work.** Run scripts/sync_vendor_cc.py FROM cmd_refresh_self_host_pin instead of printing an instruction - it updates 3 of the 5 files holding the pin and leaves the tree divergent between two commands; stamp the digest scripts/sync_github_workflow_asset.py itself wrote, so a matching dirty root is recognised as safe and syncing twice in a row is not refused because the first sync's own output looks hand-edited; add an espalier/_vendor/cc/ mirror-side branch to _reinject._render_vendor_sync so hand-editing the vendored copy of a hook warns like every other mirror in the repo.


**What this does NOT reach.** It does not reconcile the mirror ADVISORY text or the census guards - those are HYGIENE and are counted in make-every-surface-impact-obligation-name-a-real-enforcer and make-the-mirror-census-see-a-row-that-has-no-sync-script.


**Why one class.** One subsystem, one oracle: run each sync twice and edit each side of each mirror row. All three are the sync machinery either refusing a legitimate run or leaving a divergence, and each is separately provable in that one drive.


**Members (3)** — derived, never typed


| id | site | what | sev |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DEF-400b` | espalier/cli.py::cmd_refresh_self_host_pin | The self-host pin refresh rewrites its three carrier files and then only prints an instruction to run the vendor sync, leaving the two vendored copies of the pin stale. Driven at HEAD: `SELF_HOST_PIN_CARRIERS` holds three paths, the command's last statement is a print naming `scripts/sync_vendor_cc.py`, and the refresh regex matches the pin assignment in five live files — the three carriers plus `espalier/_vendor/cc/ci_guard.py` and `espalier/_vendor/cc/hooks/_self_host_fingerprint.py`. Who: the maintainer who edits `write_guard.py`, runs the refresh the in-file warning names as the remedy, and is left with a divergent tree and a red mirror until a second command. Fix shape: run the vendor sync from the command once the carriers are written, and list the vendored files beside the carriers in the report. | minor |
| `DEF-523` | scripts/sync_github_workflow_asset.py::main (the uncommitted-root refusal branch) | A second sync is refused while the root copy is dirty from the script's own first run, because nothing records what the script wrote. Driven at HEAD: the refusal fires on `asset differs from root` plus a non-clean `_root_file_status()`, and the module holds no digest or stamp of its own output — the refusal text itself concedes the case, telling the reader that if the root file is only the output of an earlier run then `--force` is the correct move. Who: the maintainer who edits the asset, syncs, edits the asset again and syncs again without committing in between, and is told their own generated file may carry a hand edit. Fix shape: record a digest of the bytes the sync writes and treat a dirty root matching it as safe, so only genuine root-side content requires `--force`. | minor |
| `DEF-419b` | tools/cc/hooks/_reinject.py::_render_vendor_sync (predicate ::_VENDOR_MIRROR_RE) | The vendor-sync advisory matches only `tools/cc/` paths, so hand-editing the vendored copy of a hook raises no warning, while the two sibling mirror families both warn on their generated side. Driven at HEAD on the real renderers: an edit to `tools/cc/hooks/write_guard.py` fires the advisory; an edit to `espalier/_vendor/cc/hooks/write_guard.py` fires nothing from any renderer; the same edit under `espalier/_vendor/selfcheck_tests/` or `espalier/assets/claude/` fires a discard warning. Who: the maintainer who edits the vendored copy and gets no signal that the next sync overwrites it from `tools/cc/`. Fix shape: add a mirror-side branch matching `espalier/_vendor/cc/` whose message leads with the discard rather than with the sync, as `_render_selfcheck_mirror_sync` does. | nit |

**What this does NOT reach.** Neither changes any output; both are measured by wall-clock before and after, and neither should be graded by correctness.


**Why one class.** Two independent hoists sharing one acceptance criterion - measure the loop before and after. This is the only class in the ledger whose value is speed, which is exactly why it is kept apart from the correctness classes rather than tucked into one.

A maintainer running the pack-fence check (or `tests/test_check_pack_fences.py`) on a tree that still holds wheel output pays that enumeration on every call. | `DEF-383b` | espalier/doctor.py::_check_python_resolver | The interpreter-resolver diagnostic calls the remedy-interpreter probe from inside its per-entry loop, so each broken entry re-runs the same uncached probe for one answer that cannot change during the call. Who: the maintainer running `espalier doctor` on a host with several mis-wired interpreter names, who waits through the probe once per entry. Fix shape: compute the remedy interpreter once before the loop — as `py3_present` already is on the line above it — and spell the local in each branch. | nit | LOGIC_BUG | MAINTAINER |

### §C30 — Derive every pack pre-flight obligation from the enforcer that would actually red

**Unit of work.** One pass over espalier/surface_impact.py: import espalier/mirror_registry.py and render every mirror advisory from its rows - VERIFIED, the registry is imported by tests only, so a row can declare the wrong direction and every guard stays green; give classify_surface an optional repo_root and consult mirror_registry.covers() so editing a curated mirror's SOURCE side names the sync script that must run; add docs/QUICKSTART.md to _hook_demands' hook-count site list, since QUICKSTART's 'twelve entry scripts' is the one hook-count claim no test pins; add a top-level tools/ branch plus an EXPECTED_ count and scan-root entry, since that tree is governed by none of the five surface mechanisms yet carries a CI job; pin the advisory's prefix set and verb demand to their enforcers' actual preconditions; and rewrite the advisory that contradicts _reinject's about the same sync script so both name its two refusal arms.


**What this does NOT reach.** It cannot fix the tools/cc side of any advisory by import - tools/cc/ may not import espalier, so the same repair lands twice and the mirror syncs must run. It also does not emit obligations for DELETED paths (emit-obligations-for-what-a-pack-deletes-or-renames), which is a LOGIC_BUG and counted separately.


**Why one class.** One file and one acceptance rule that closes every member: for each obligation the report emits, break the named thing and require the named enforcer to red; for each enforcer, require the report to name it. DEF-607's false demand has already propagated into authored pack text, which is the evidence that the emitter - not any single obligation - is the unit of work.

### §C33 — Resolve every bench-corpus reference field and combo segment, then repoint the one that fails

**Unit of work.** In tests/test_corpus_documented_in_resolves.py resolve every ref field and every combo segment INCLUDING the out-of-scope rows - the gate skips 9 of 53 rows, reads 2 of 9 fields, and green-lights a multi-part reference on its first resolving fragment; then repoint the one bench corpus row whose documented_in names documentation that does not exist, and drop the out-of-scope exclusion.


**What this does NOT reach.** It is the bench corpus only; it does not resolve doc citations (teach-the-citation-resolvers-every-anchor-shape-the-repo-writes).


**Why one class.** The widening EXPOSES the instance: DEF-418b's excluded out-of-scope class is exactly where DEF-526's dead documented_in lives. Landing them apart means either landing the gate red or fixing an instance no gate protects. One pack, one red-then-green.


_Both independent reclassification passes grouped these members._


**Members (2)** — derived, never typed


| id | site | what | sev |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DEF-418b` | tests/test_corpus_documented_in_resolves.py::TestCorpusDocumentedIn::test_documented_in_resolves (:40, the OOS-excluding parametrize) + tests/_corpus_ref_resolver.py::_resolve_test_ref (:53) | The bench-corpus reference gate resolves one field over part of the corpus and green-lights a combo reference on its first resolving fragment. Driven at HEAD: the parametrize excludes the 8 `BC-OOS-*` rows of 65; it reads `documented_in` with a `regression_test` fallback no row carries and never resolves `fix_module`, which 54 rows declare and four of which still name `tools/cc/hooks/write_guard.py` as the home of `_DD_OF_RE`, `_TAR_C_RE`, `_INSTALL_CMD_RE` and `_TRUNCATE_CMD_RE` — all four now in `tools/cc/hooks/_bash_patterns.py`; and `_resolve_test_ref` returns True on the first resolving segment, so a combo reference with a dead fragment passes. A contributor adding a corpus row is told by a green run that its references resolve, when only one of them was read. | `DEF-653` | bench/run_benchmark.py::invoke_real_write_guard (:191) and ::_extract_block_reason (:510) + tests/test_bench_corpus_schema.py::_REQUIRED_TOP_KEYS (:26) + bench/README.md:272,:293 | `expected_block_reason` is published in `bench/README.md` as a corpus schema field and no Python in the repo reads it. Driven at HEAD: all 65 `bench/corpus/BC-*.json` rows declare it across 383 attempts, a search of every tracked `.py` finds zero consumers, `_REQUIRED_TOP_KEYS` does not name it, and `invoke_real_write_guard` captures the hook's real `permissionDecisionReason` through `_extract_block_reason` and then scores the attempt on the deny bit alone — so a class can pass on a guard other than the one it exists to pin. Unread, the declared values have drifted into 111 distinct strings mixing reasons the hook actually prints with slugs like `protected_harness_zone` that it never prints. The operator gates a release on the in-scope-blocked row of `bench/RESULTS.md`, which `.github/workflows/benchmark.yml` re-runs on every PR, and a contributor adding a class copies the nearest neighbour's reason string and ships a value the hook never emits. Fix shape: decide first whether the field is a contract, then either assert it against the captured reason for blocked attempts and repair the rows that red, or delete it from the README schema and the corpus — not both halves at once. | minor |

### §C34 — Derive mirror families from the byte-parity assertions so a row without a sync script is visible

**Unit of work.** Derive candidate mirror families in tests/test_reinject_sync.py from tests/ byte-parity assertions rather than from sync scripts - a family that arrives without one, as harness-guard did, is invisible to every census check; guard the unguarded split with has_sync and reword the misdescribing assertion message, so adding a sync-less row reports the real problem instead of crashing with a confusing error; assert every curated mirror name is slash-free in the parity test, so a subdirectory cannot silence the edit warning for every file inside it.


**What this does NOT reach.** It does not fix the sync scripts themselves (make-every-sync-command-leave-the-tree-converged) or the advisory text (make-every-surface-impact-obligation-name-a-real-enforcer).


**Why one class.** One test module, one acceptance test: add a synthetic sync-less mirror row with a subdirectory and require the census to see it and report the real problem. All three members are the same blind spot at three depths of the same guard.


_Both independent reclassification passes grouped these members._


**Members (3)** — derived, never typed


| id | site | what | sev |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DEF-419c` | tests/test_reinject_sync.py::TestMirrorCensusCoverage::test_every_tracked_sync_script_is_claimed_by_a_row (:462) | The one completeness oracle over the mirror registry keys on sync scripts: it diffs `git ls-files scripts/sync_*.py` against the scripts the rows claim, so a mirror family that arrives with no sync script — a hand-copied pair — is claimed by nothing and seen by nothing, and every other assertion in the module iterates `MIRROR_ROWS` and is structurally incapable of noticing an undeclared family. A maintainer who adds a byte-mirrored pair by hand and skips the registry row gets a green suite, no census coverage and no edit advisory on either side of the pair. Fix shape: derive the candidate families from the tree's byte-parity assertions rather than from the sync scripts, so a family without one is still a candidate the census must account for. | nit |
| `DEF-477` | tests/test_reinject_sync.py::TestMirrorCensusCoverage::test_every_row_has_a_surface_impact_obligation (:994, the unguarded split) and ::test_every_row_remedy_names_its_real_sync (:1027, the misdescribing message) | Declaring a mirror registry row with no sync script raises inside the test instead of reporting the problem: `test_every_row_has_a_surface_impact_obligation` calls `r.sync.split()[-1]` with no `has_sync` guard — its two sibling sites at :820 and :909 both have one — and `test_every_row_remedy_names_its_real_sync` then asserts `has_sync` with the message "lost its sync script", which misdescribes a row that never had one. `MirrorRow` accepts `sync=None` and the module's own synthetic row uses it, so the shape is reachable, not hypothetical. A maintainer declaring a hand-copied mirror family reads an attribute error and a wrong diagnosis instead of "this row needs a sync script or an explicit copy-by-hand remedy". Fix shape: guard the split with `has_sync` and reword the assertion to name the real condition. | nit |
| `DEF-419e` | tools/cc/hooks/_reinject.py::_SELFCHECK_SOT_RE (:415, the flatness comment at :413-414) | The selfcheck mirror's edit advisory can only recognise a direct child of `tests/`: `_SELFCHECK_SOT_RE`'s name class excludes the slash, and the comment above it states that flatness as construction rather than as a pinned contract. Nothing asserts it — neither `tests/test_selfcheck_tests_parity.py` nor the mirror census in `tests/test_reinject_sync.py` requires a curated mirror name to be slash-free — so the day the mirror grows a subdirectory the warning goes silent for every file inside it and the suite stays green. A maintainer editing a mirrored test under that subdirectory is never told the edit will be discarded by the next sync run. Fix shape: assert in the parity test that every curated mirror name contains no slash, so a subdirectory reds instead of quietly widening the blind spot. | minor |

### §C35 — Derive the cross-boundary duplicate register, then collapse what can collapse and pin what cannot

**Unit of work.** AST-derive the equal-valued pair set across the tools/cc <-> espalier no-import boundary and force each pair into _PINNED or a marker - the hand-kept register misses 8 of 26 duplicated names; then take the two collapses it exposes: delete _recall._split_sections and call _hook_utils.iter_doc_sections (two copies of one markdown section-splitter feeding one 'single owner' claim, and neither ignores headings inside fenced code blocks), and hoist the subagent-marker literal into _blueprint_limits.py in both trees, where four definitions exist and only two are forced by the boundary.


**What this does NOT reach.** It cannot collapse the pairs the import boundary FORCES - those must end up pinned, not merged. Census caveat to carry: the row's own example _AJ_MAX_FIELD_CHARS is now pinned, and CONVENTIONS' parity table holds 5 rows, not the 7 the row cites.


**Why one class.** Derive the population first, then act on it - that is one unit of work, and doing the two collapses without the register is exactly how the register came to miss 8 names. One acceptance criterion: every equal-valued cross-boundary pair is either collapsed or pinned, with no third state.


_Both independent reclassification passes grouped these members._


**Members (3)** — derived, never typed


| id | site | what | sev |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DEF-549` | tests/test_forced_copy_parity.py (the hand-written register) + docs/CONVENTIONS.md#library--hook-parity | The register of constants duplicated across the `tools/cc/` <-> `espalier/` no-import boundary is hand-kept, so a forced duplicate nobody remembers to add is pinned by nothing. Driven at HEAD: an AST census of module-level assignments finds 32 names defined in both trees with at least one equal value, while `tests/test_forced_copy_parity.py` is fifteen hand-written parity classes and the library/hook parity table in `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` holds five rows; none of `LOCAL_LINK_RE`, `MAX_FRESHNESS_MANIFEST_BYTES`, `STATE_CACHE_MAX_AGE_HOURS`, `_AJ_PROSE_FIELDS`, `_ANCESTOR_WINDOW_COMMITS`, `_ATX_HEADING`, `_MD_LINK_TEXT` or `_SLUG_DROP` appears anywhere under `tests/`. A maintainer editing one copy of an unpinned pair ships a silent divergence across the very boundary the duplication exists to protect. Fix shape: derive the equal-valued pair set by AST and force every pair into the register or an explicit marker, so there is no third state. | minor | HYGIENE | MAINTAINER |
| `DEF-588` | `tools/cc/reflect_protocol.py::_ACTIVITY_LOG_PREFIX` (:393) + `tools/cc/cognitive_blueprint.py::_ACTIVITY_LOG_PREFIX` (:628) + `espalier/cognitive_blueprint.py::_ACTIVITY_LOG_PREFIX` (:569) + `espalier/scaffolding_canon.py::_SUBAGENT_PREFIX` (:33) | The subagent activity-log marker is defined at four sites across the two trees and only one of the two pairs is forced by the no-import boundary: the `tools/cc/` pair and the `espalier/` pair are each an untaken same-tree collapse. Who: the maintainer changing the marker or its readers, who has to find four owners where two would do. Fix: collapse each tree's pair onto one owner so the only remaining duplicate is the cross-boundary one, which `tests/test_cognitive_blueprint.py::TestActivityLogFilterReachesEveryReader::test_every_consumer_recognizes_what_the_producer_writes` already holds to the producer's spelling. | nit | HYGIENE | MAINTAINER |
| `DEF-664` | `tools/cc/hooks/_hook_utils.py::_project_root_spelling` (:287) vs `tools/cc/_paths.py::_repo_root` (:39) and `tools/cc/statusline.py::main` (:192) | Two spellings of "resolve the repo root" disagree: `_project_root_spelling` translates a Git Bash `/c/...` value through `_msys_drive_to_windows` and warns only when the project-directory variable is the empty string, while `_paths._repo_root` and `statusline.main` resolve that variable raw, with no drive translation and no warning when it is unset. Who: the Windows adopter driving the hooks from Git Bash, whose plan is written under the raw root, is not found under the translated one and has every Write and Edit refused because the plan gate fails closed; and anyone driving a hook outside Claude Code with the variable unset, whose session state lands silently in a cwd-rooted state directory the caps never read. Fix: one resolver with one translation, called by both trees' entry points, rather than a gate on each spelling. | minor | LOGIC_BUG | ADOPTER |

<a id="c36"></a>

### §C36 — For each named assertion, write the mutation it claims to catch, watch it pass, then tighten until it reds

**Unit of work.** Seven guards, each closed the same way: assert set EQUALITY not membership in the rc=0 gate-status derivation, so an extra status cannot silently widen two CLI exit codes; probe every verb regex in test_write_guard, not only the ones that already carry the guard, so a new pattern that forgets it is visible; compare leaf CONTAINER TYPE per path in tests/_json_key_paths.py so a documented sample cannot collapse a whole dict into a single word; allowlist real commands and require a target-shaped subject field in the denial-actionability check, which any stray file path currently satisfies; seed the autoprune fixture DESCENDING and assert archived rows by date, since 'oldest' and 'first' are the same row today; assert the smaller deny-token list is a subset of the larger, pinning the three-token delta - by asserting, NEVER by widening the smaller list; and add a golden-string test over _explain_path.render() so a silent reword of what /status --explain prints is noticed.


**What this does NOT reach.** It closes only the SEVEN named guards. It does not enumerate the population - no scanner finds weak assertions - so the member list is the whole claim, and a pack must not report 'the class of weak assertions is closed'.


**Why one class.** The repairs differ per member but the ACCEPTANCE CRITERION is identical and mechanical: introduce the defect the guard claims to catch, confirm it passes today, tighten until it reds. That shared, per-member-provable criterion is what lets one pack honestly report every member closed - it is one method applied seven times, explicitly not one fix.


**Members (11)** — derived, never typed

Who: the maintainer who adds a non-failure status and silently makes the self-host check and the pre-release gate return 0 on a case nobody reviewed. Fix: assert set equality against the shared frozenset instead of membership. | nit | HYGIENE | MAINTAINER |

### §C37 — Replace four hand-kept gate populations with a derivation from the live source

**Unit of work.** Add a numeric-contract site enumerator reading the freshness cache plus the MIGRATIONS tuple, so re-pinning any count has a findable second site instead of reddening the suite with no pointer; derive tests/README.md's local-runs/CI-skips list from live skip reasons, since it names 4 files when at least 8 are gated that way and the majority of the class sits inside the blind spot the section exists to expose; derive the QUICKSTART banner test's LIVE_LABELS from the driven session_start output, since a hand-kept tuple reads a genuinely-new label as 'not emitted' and blocks a correct doc update; widen test_self_hosting's _reads_the_status predicate to the second spelling that status is written under, so a fifth copy cannot be added without reddening anything.


**What this does NOT reach.** It does not derive the record-surface registries (point-the-remaining-record-surface-registries-at-the-canon) or the mirror families (make-the-mirror-census-see-a-row-that-has-no-sync-script) - those have their own canons and their own packs.


**Why one class.** One repair verb with one acceptance criterion per member: add a real member to the live population and require the derived list to pick it up without an edit. Four gates, four sources, one sitting.


_Both independent reclassification passes grouped these members._

### §C51 — Make the forward work a shipped surface: rebuild the ledger to forward work only, fold the findings corpus into it, and move the shipping boundary and the codename gate with it

**Unit of work.** `DEC-31`'s pack: the forward work becomes a tracked, public surface of the
repo that hosts development, and everything that only made sense while it was private moves
with it in one pass — the ledger is rebuilt to forward work only (`DEF-705`), the findings
corpus folds into it (`DEF-706`), the shipping boundary flips for the ledger, its probes file
and the active packs (`DEF-707`), and the codename gate grows the arm that enforces a sensitive
name without shipping it (`DEF-708`). One pack can honestly report the class closed because the
seed is the acceptance test: the built archive carries the rebuilt ledger and nothing from the
internal set.

**What this does NOT reach.** The DEC-25 texts that describe the old plan are §C48's
(`DEF-709`); the record branch's push is a one-line chore in §C0 (`DEF-710`); Done, Deferred,
Merged, Scrapped, blueprints and reports stay untracked, regenerable history on the record
branch, by the operator's call on 2026-09-07.

**Why one class.** Four edits, one acceptance test, one moment: all four must land before the
seed and none is useful alone — a rebuilt ledger that still classifies `local_only` never
ships, a public ledger that still carries §7 ships the war stories, a fold without the gate
ships the scrub list. Sequenced at the start of the release-repo phase, after the
adopter-facing lanes, with the cut mechanical enough to re-run at seed time.

Who: the maintainer running the strike check in the public checkout, who strikes live work on "the probe lost its footing".
